## Transcript

00:00:00 --> 00:00:01
- The great lie we tell ourselves,

00:00:00 --> 00:00:03
is that people who are evil

00:00:02 --> 00:00:04
are not like us.

00:00:03 --> 00:00:05
They're a class apart.

00:00:04 --> 00:00:07
Everybody in history who has sinned is a person

00:00:06 --> 00:00:09
who's very different from me.

00:00:08 --> 00:00:11
Robert George, the philosopher over at Princeton,

00:00:10 --> 00:00:13
he's fond of doing sort of thought experiment

00:00:12 --> 00:00:14
in his classes where he asks

00:00:13 --> 00:00:17
people to raise their hand if they had lived in Alabama

00:00:16 --> 00:00:20
in 1861, "How many of you would be abolitionists?"

00:00:19 --> 00:00:21
And everybody raises their hand and he says,

00:00:20 --> 00:00:23
"Of course that's not true.

00:00:22 --> 00:00:25
"Of course, that's not true."

00:00:24 --> 00:00:29
The best protection against evil is recognizing that it lies

00:00:28 --> 00:00:31
in every human heart and the possibility

00:00:30 --> 00:00:33
that it takes you over.

00:00:32 --> 00:00:35
- Do you ever sit back,

00:00:35 --> 00:00:38
in the quiet of your mind and think,

00:00:37 --> 00:00:39
Am I participating in evil?

00:00:41 --> 00:00:45
The following is a conversation with Ben Shapiro,

00:00:44 --> 00:00:47
a conservative political commentator,

00:00:46 --> 00:00:49
host of the Ben Shapiro Show,

00:00:48 --> 00:00:51
co-founder of The Daily Wire,

00:00:50 --> 00:00:53
and author of several books,

00:00:52 --> 00:00:55
including The Authoritarian Moment,

00:00:54 --> 00:00:57
The Right Side of History,

00:00:56 --> 00:01:00
and Facts Don't Care About Your Feelings.

00:00:59 --> 00:01:02
Whatever your political leanings,

00:01:01 --> 00:01:06
I humbly ask that you try to put those aside and listen

00:01:05 --> 00:01:08
with an open mind,

00:01:07 --> 00:01:10
trying to give the most charitable interpretation

00:01:09 --> 00:01:12
of the words we say.

00:01:11 --> 00:01:15
This is true in general for this podcast.

00:01:14 --> 00:01:17
Whether the guest is Ben Shapiro

00:01:16 --> 00:01:20
r Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez,

00:01:19 --> 00:01:24
Donald Trump or Barack Obama.

00:01:23 --> 00:01:27
I will talk to everyone from every side,

00:01:26 --> 00:01:29
from the far left to the far right,

00:01:28 --> 00:01:33
from presidents to prisoners, from artists to scientists,

00:01:32 --> 00:01:35
from the powerful to the powerless.

00:01:34 --> 00:01:40
Because we are all human, all capable of good and evil,

00:01:39 --> 00:01:45
all with fascinating stories and ideas to explore.

00:01:44 --> 00:01:48
I seek only to understand, and in so doing,

00:01:47 --> 00:01:51
hopefully add a bit of love to the world.

00:01:51 --> 00:01:54
This is Lex Fridman Podcast, to support it,

00:01:54 --> 00:01:57
please check out our sponsors in the description.

00:01:56 --> 00:02:01
And now, dear friends, here's Ben Shapiro.

00:02:01 --> 00:02:04
Let's start with a difficult topic.

00:02:04 --> 00:02:07
What do you think about the comments made by Ye,

00:02:06 --> 00:02:10
formerly known as Kanye West, about Jewish people?

00:02:09 --> 00:02:13
- They're awful and antisemitic,

00:02:12 --> 00:02:15
and they seem to get worse over time.

00:02:14 --> 00:02:19
They started off with the bizarre death con 3 tweet,

00:02:18 --> 00:02:24
and then they went into even more stereotypical garbage

00:02:23 --> 00:02:27
about Jews and Jews being sexual manipulators.

00:02:26 --> 00:02:29
I think that was the Pete Davidson, Kim Kardashian stuff.

00:02:28 --> 00:02:32
And then, Jews running all of the media,

00:02:31 --> 00:02:35
Jews being in charge of the financial sector, Jewish people.

00:02:34 --> 00:02:37
I mean, there's no, I mean,

00:02:36 --> 00:02:39
I called it on my show, there's Sherman Nazism,

00:02:38 --> 00:02:40
and it is,

00:02:39 --> 00:02:41
it's like right

00:02:40 --> 00:02:44
from Protocols of the Elders of Zion type stuff.

00:02:43 --> 00:02:46
- Do you think those words come from pain,

00:02:45 --> 00:02:48
where do they come from?

00:02:47 --> 00:02:50
- It's always hard to try and read somebody's mind,

00:02:49 --> 00:02:51
what he looks like,

00:02:50 --> 00:02:53
to me, just having experience in my own family

00:02:52 --> 00:02:55
with people who are bipolar is,

00:02:54 --> 00:02:56
he seems like a bipolar personality.

00:02:55 --> 00:02:59
He seems like somebody who is in the middle

00:02:58 --> 00:03:01
of a manic episode.

00:03:00 --> 00:03:04
And when you're manic you tend to say a lot of things

00:03:03 --> 00:03:06
that you shouldn't say,

00:03:05 --> 00:03:08
and you tend to believe that they're the most brilliant

00:03:07 --> 00:03:09
hings ever said.

00:03:08 --> 00:03:10
The Washington Post,

00:03:09 --> 00:03:11
did an entire piece

00:03:10 --> 00:03:13
speculating about how bipolarism played

00:03:12 --> 00:03:16
into the kind of stuff that Ye was saying.

00:03:15 --> 00:03:21
And it's hard for me to think that it's not playing into it,

00:03:20 --> 00:03:25
especially because even if he is an anti-Semite,

00:03:24 --> 00:03:26
and I have no reason to suspect he's not,

00:03:25 --> 00:03:28
given all of his comments.

00:03:27 --> 00:03:30
If he had an ounce of common sense,

00:03:29 --> 00:03:31
he would stop at a certain point.

00:03:30 --> 00:03:34
And bipolarism tends to drive you well past

00:03:33 --> 00:03:38
he point where common sense applies.

00:03:37 --> 00:03:41
So, I would imagine it's coming from that.

00:03:41 --> 00:03:43
From his comments,

00:03:42 --> 00:03:47
I would also imagine that he's doing the logical mistake,

00:03:46 --> 00:03:49
that a lot of anti-Semites or racist or bigots do;

00:03:48 --> 00:03:53
which is somebody hurt me, that person is a Jew,

00:03:52 --> 00:03:55
therefore all Jews are bad.

00:03:54 --> 00:03:58
And,cthat jump from a person did something to me,

00:03:57 --> 00:03:59
I don't like,

00:03:58 --> 00:04:02
who's a member of a particular race or class,

00:04:01 --> 00:04:05
and therefore everybody of that race or class is bad.

00:04:04 --> 00:04:06
That's textbook bigotry

00:04:05 --> 00:04:08
and that's pretty obviously what Ye's engaging in here.

00:04:07 --> 00:04:10
- So jumping from the individual to the group.

00:04:09 --> 00:04:11
That's the way he's been expressing it, right.

00:04:10 --> 00:04:12
He keeps talking about his Jewish agents,

00:04:11 --> 00:04:14
and I watched your interview with him and you kept saying,

00:04:13 --> 00:04:15
"So just name the agents."

00:04:14 --> 00:04:18
Just name the people who are, who are screwing you.

00:04:17 --> 00:04:19
And he wouldn't do it.

00:04:18 --> 00:04:21
Instead, he just kept going back to the general,

00:04:20 --> 00:04:23
the group, the Jews in general.

00:04:22 --> 00:04:26
I mean, that's textbook bigotry.

00:04:25 --> 00:04:28
And, if we're putting any other context,

00:04:27 --> 00:04:30
he would probably recognize it as such.

00:04:30 --> 00:04:34
- To the degree is worth fuel hate in the world.

00:04:33 --> 00:04:36
What's the way to reverse that process?

00:04:35 --> 00:04:38
What's the way to alleviate the hate?

00:04:37 --> 00:04:41
- I mean, when it comes to alleviating the kind of stuff

00:04:40 --> 00:04:44
that he's saying, obviously debunking it.

00:04:45 --> 00:04:48
Making clear that what he's saying is garbage.

00:04:48 --> 00:04:54
But, the reality is that I think that for most people

00:04:53 --> 00:04:57
who are in any way engaged with these issues,

00:04:56 --> 00:04:58
I don't think they're being convinced

00:04:57 --> 00:05:01
to be antisemitic by Ye.

00:05:00 --> 00:05:03
I think that there's a group of people who may be swayed

00:05:02 --> 00:05:05
that antisemitism is acceptable

00:05:04 --> 00:05:07
because Ye is saying what he's saying,

00:05:06 --> 00:05:09
and he's saying so very loudly,

00:05:08 --> 00:05:11
and he's saying it over and over.

00:05:10 --> 00:05:13
But yeah, I think that for example,

00:05:12 --> 00:05:16
there were these signs that were popping up in Los Angeles

00:05:15 --> 00:05:17
aying, Ye is right.

00:05:16 --> 00:05:19
Well, that group's been out there posting antisemitic

00:05:18 --> 00:05:21
signs on the freeways for years.

00:05:20 --> 00:05:23
And, there are groups like that

00:05:22 --> 00:05:24
posting antisemitic signs where I live in Florida.

00:05:24 --> 00:05:26
They've been doing that for years.

00:05:25 --> 00:05:27
Well before YA was saying this sort of stuff,

00:05:26 --> 00:05:28
it's just like latest opportunity

00:05:27 --> 00:05:30
to kind of jump on on that particular bandwagon.

00:05:29 --> 00:05:34
But listen, I think that people do have a moral duty

00:05:33 --> 00:05:35
to call that stuff out.

00:05:34 --> 00:05:38
- So there is a degree to which it normalizes

00:05:37 --> 00:05:42
that kind of idea that Jews control the media,

00:05:41 --> 00:05:44
Jews control X institution.

00:05:45 --> 00:05:50
Is there a way to talk about

00:05:49 --> 00:05:55
a high representation of a group like Jewish people

00:05:54 --> 00:05:58
in a certain institution like the media

00:05:57 --> 00:06:00
or Hollywood and so on,

00:05:59 --> 00:06:03
without it being a hateful conversation?

00:06:02 --> 00:06:04
- Sure, of course.

00:06:03 --> 00:06:08
A high percentage of, higher than statistically represented,

00:06:07 --> 00:06:09
in the population,

00:06:08 --> 00:06:12
percentage of Hollywood agents are probably Jewish.

00:06:11 --> 00:06:14
A higher percentage of lawyers generally

00:06:13 --> 00:06:15
are probably Jewish.

00:06:14 --> 00:06:17
A high percentage of accountants are probably Jewish.

00:06:16 --> 00:06:19
Also, a higher percentage of of engineers

00:06:18 --> 00:06:21
are probably Asian.

00:06:20 --> 00:06:23
Statistical truths are statistical truths.

00:06:22 --> 00:06:28
It doesn't necessarily mean anything about the nature

00:06:27 --> 00:06:30
of the people who are being talked about.

00:06:29 --> 00:06:31
They're a myriad of reasons

00:06:30 --> 00:06:33
why people might be disproportionately

00:06:32 --> 00:06:35
in one arena or another;

00:06:34 --> 00:06:38
ranging from the cultural to sometimes the genetic.

00:06:37 --> 00:06:40
There are certain areas of the world

00:06:39 --> 00:06:42
where people are better long distance runners

00:06:41 --> 00:06:44
because of their genetic adaptations

00:06:43 --> 00:06:45
in those particular areas of the world.

00:06:44 --> 00:06:46
That's not racist.

00:06:45 --> 00:06:48
That's just fact.

00:06:47 --> 00:06:49
What starts to get racist

00:06:48 --> 00:06:51
is when you are attributing a bad characteristic

00:06:50 --> 00:06:56
to an entire population based on the notion

00:06:55 --> 00:06:58
that some members of that population

00:06:57 --> 00:06:59
are doing bad things.

00:06:58 --> 00:07:01
- Yeah, there's a jump between.

00:07:00 --> 00:07:04
It's also possible that record label owners

00:07:03 --> 00:07:09
as a group have a kind of culture that F's over artists.

00:07:09 --> 00:07:11
- Sure.

00:07:10 --> 00:07:12
- Doesn't treat artists fairly.

00:07:11 --> 00:07:14
And, it's also possible that there's a high representation

00:07:13 --> 00:07:19
of Jews in the group of people that own record labels,

00:07:19 --> 00:07:21
But, it's that small,

00:07:20 --> 00:07:25
but a very big leap that people take from the group

00:07:24 --> 00:07:28
that own record labels to all Jews.

00:07:27 --> 00:07:29
- For sure.

00:07:28 --> 00:07:33
I think that one of the other issues also is that

00:07:32 --> 00:07:35
antisemitism is fascinating

00:07:34 --> 00:07:37
because it breaks down into so many different parts.

00:07:36 --> 00:07:39
Meaning that, if you look at sort of different types

00:07:38 --> 00:07:42
of antisemitism, if you're a racist against black people,

00:07:41 --> 00:07:44
it's typically because you're racist based on the color

00:07:43 --> 00:07:45
of their skin.

00:07:44 --> 00:07:48
If you're racist, against the Jews, you're antisemitic.

00:07:47 --> 00:07:49
Then, there are actually a few different ways

00:07:48 --> 00:07:50
that breaks down, right.

00:07:49 --> 00:07:52
You have antisemitism in terms of ethnicity;

00:07:51 --> 00:07:55
which is like Nazi-esk antisemitism.

00:07:54 --> 00:07:58
You have Jewish parentage, you have a Jewish grandparent,

00:07:57 --> 00:07:59
therefore you are,

00:07:58 --> 00:08:00
your blood is corrupt

00:07:59 --> 00:08:02
and you are inherently going to have bad properties.

00:08:01 --> 00:08:05
Then, there's sort of old school religious antisemitism;

00:08:04 --> 00:08:08
which is that the Jews are the killers of Christ,

00:08:07 --> 00:08:10
or the Jews are the sons of pigs and monkeys,

00:08:09 --> 00:08:13
and therefore Judaism is bad and therefore Jews are bad.

00:08:12 --> 00:08:15
And, the way that you get out of that antisemitism

00:08:14 --> 00:08:17
historically speaking is mass conversion;

00:08:16 --> 00:08:20
which most antisemitism for a couple thousand years actually

00:08:19 --> 00:08:21
was not ethnic,

00:08:20 --> 00:08:24
it was much more rooted in this sort of stuff, right.

00:08:23 --> 00:08:26
If a Jew converted out of the faith,

00:08:25 --> 00:08:29
then the antisemitism was quote unquote alleviated.

00:08:28 --> 00:08:33
And then, there's a sort of bizarre antisemitism

00:08:32 --> 00:08:34
that's political antisemitism,

00:08:33 --> 00:08:38
and that is members of a group that I don't like

00:08:37 --> 00:08:39
are disproportionately Jewish.

00:08:39 --> 00:08:44
Therefore all Jews are members of this group

00:08:44 --> 00:08:47
or are predominantly represented in this group.

00:08:46 --> 00:08:50
So, you'll see Nazis saying the communists are Jews.

00:08:49 --> 00:08:51
You'll see communist saying the Nazis are Jews,

00:08:50 --> 00:08:54
or you'll see communist saying that the capitalists

00:08:53 --> 00:08:55
rather are Jews.

00:08:54 --> 00:08:57
And, so that's the weird thing about antisemitism.

00:08:56 --> 00:08:59
It's kind of like the Jews behind every corner.

00:08:58 --> 00:09:01
It's basically a big conspiracy theory.

00:09:00 --> 00:09:02
Unlike a lot of other forms of racism;

00:09:01 --> 00:09:04
which are not really conspiracy theory,

00:09:03 --> 00:09:06
antisemitism tends to be a conspiracy theory

00:09:05 --> 00:09:07
about the levers of power

00:09:06 --> 00:09:10
being controlled by a shadowy cadre of people

00:09:09 --> 00:09:12
who are getting together behind closed doors

00:09:11 --> 00:09:13
to control things.

00:09:12 --> 00:09:16
- Yeah, the most absurd illustration of antisemitism,

00:09:15 --> 00:09:21
just like you said, is Stalin versus Hitler over Poland.

00:09:20 --> 00:09:25
That every bad guy was a Jew, right.

00:09:24 --> 00:09:27
It was like, every enemy,

00:09:26 --> 00:09:29
there's a lot of different enemy groups, intellectuals,

00:09:28 --> 00:09:30
political and so on.

00:09:29 --> 00:09:34
Military, and behind any movement that is considered

00:09:33 --> 00:09:36
the enemy for the Nazis,

00:09:35 --> 00:09:38
and any movement that's considered the enemy

00:09:37 --> 00:09:42
for the Soviet army are the Jews.

00:09:41 --> 00:09:45
What is the fact that Hitler took power

00:09:45 --> 00:09:49
teach you about human nature?

00:09:48 --> 00:09:51
When you look back at the history of the 20th century,

00:09:50 --> 00:09:53
what do you learn from that time?

00:09:52 --> 00:09:54
- I mean, there are a bunch of lessons

00:09:54 --> 00:09:56
to Hitler taking power.

00:09:55 --> 00:09:59
The first thing, I think people ought to recognize

00:09:58 --> 00:10:00
about Hitler taking power

00:09:59 --> 00:10:02
is that the power had been centralized

00:10:01 --> 00:10:04
in the government before Hitler took it.

00:10:03 --> 00:10:06
If you actually look at the history of Nazi Germany,

00:10:05 --> 00:10:09
the Weimar Republic had effectively collapsed.

00:10:08 --> 00:10:12
The power had been centralized in the Chancellery,

00:10:12 --> 00:10:15
and really under Hindenburg

00:10:14 --> 00:10:17
for a couple of years before that.

00:10:16 --> 00:10:20
And so, it was only a matter of time until someone

00:10:19 --> 00:10:22
who was bad grabbed the power.

00:10:21 --> 00:10:24
And so, the struggle between the reds and the browns

00:10:23 --> 00:10:28
in Nazism, in pre-Nazi Germany,

00:10:27 --> 00:10:32
led to this kind of up spiraling of radical sentiment

00:10:31 --> 00:10:34
hat allowed Hitler in through the front door,

00:10:33 --> 00:10:35
not through the back door.

00:10:34 --> 00:10:36
He was elected.

00:10:35 --> 00:10:38
- So you think communists could have also taken power.

00:10:37 --> 00:10:39
- I mean, there's no question communists

00:10:38 --> 00:10:40
could have taken power.

00:10:39 --> 00:10:42
They were a serious force in in pre-Nazi Germany.

00:10:41 --> 00:10:44
- Do you think there was an underlying current

00:10:43 --> 00:10:46
hat would've led to an atrocity,

00:10:45 --> 00:10:48
if the communist had taken power?

00:10:47 --> 00:10:49
- Wouldn't have been quite the same atrocity,

00:10:48 --> 00:10:51
but obviously the communists in Soviet Russia,

00:10:50 --> 00:10:53
at exactly this time were, were committing the Holodomor.

00:10:52 --> 00:10:54
- Yeah.

00:10:53 --> 00:10:57
- So it was, there were very few good guys

00:10:56 --> 00:10:58
in terms of good parties.

00:10:57 --> 00:11:01
The moderate parties were being dragged by the radicals

00:11:00 --> 00:11:03
into alliance with them

00:11:02 --> 00:11:06
to prevent the worst case scenario from the other guy.

00:11:05 --> 00:11:07
So if you look at,

00:11:06 --> 00:11:10
I'm sort of fascinated by the history of this period,

00:11:09 --> 00:11:12
because it really does speak to,

00:11:11 --> 00:11:14
how does a democracy break down?

00:11:13 --> 00:11:15
I mean, the 20's,

00:11:14 --> 00:11:17
Weimar Republic was a very liberal democracy.

00:11:16 --> 00:11:18
How does a liberal democracy break down

00:11:17 --> 00:11:22
into complete fascism, and then into genocide?

00:11:21 --> 00:11:25
And, there's a character who was very prominent

00:11:24 --> 00:11:27
in the history at that time named Franz von Papen,

00:11:26 --> 00:11:30
who was actually the second to last chancellor

00:11:29 --> 00:11:31
of the Republic before Hitler.

00:11:30 --> 00:11:32
So he was the chancellor,

00:11:31 --> 00:11:33
and then he handed over to Schleicher.

00:11:32 --> 00:11:36
And then, he ended up, Schleicher ended up collapsing,

00:11:35 --> 00:11:38
and that ended up handing power over to Hitler.

00:11:37 --> 00:11:40
It was Papen who had stumped for Hitler

00:11:39 --> 00:11:42
to become chancellor.

00:11:41 --> 00:11:45
Papen was a Catholic Democrat.

00:11:44 --> 00:11:46
He didn't like Hitler.

00:11:45 --> 00:11:49
He thought that Hitler was a radical and a nut job,

00:11:48 --> 00:11:52
but he also thought that Hitler being a buffoon,

00:11:51 --> 00:11:53
as he saw it,

00:11:52 --> 00:11:57
was going to essentially be usable by the right forces

00:11:57 --> 00:12:01
in order to prevent the communists from taking power.

00:12:00 --> 00:12:03
Maybe in order to restore some sort of legitimacy

00:12:02 --> 00:12:06
to the regime because he was popular.

00:12:05 --> 00:12:07
In order for Papen

00:12:06 --> 00:12:09
to retain power himself.

00:12:08 --> 00:12:12
And then, immediately after Hitler taking power,

00:12:11 --> 00:12:13
Hitler basically kills all of Papen's friends.

00:12:12 --> 00:12:16
Papen out of quote unquote loyalty, stays on.

00:12:15 --> 00:12:19
He ends up helping the Anschluss in Austria.

00:12:18 --> 00:12:20
All this stuff is really interesting,

00:12:19 --> 00:12:23
mainly because what it speaks to is the great lie

00:12:22 --> 00:12:25
we tell ourselves that people who are evil

00:12:24 --> 00:12:25
are not like us.

00:12:24 --> 00:12:26
They're a class apart.

00:12:25 --> 00:12:27
People who do evil things,

00:12:26 --> 00:12:30
people who support evil people; they're not like us.

00:12:29 --> 00:12:32
That's an easy call.

00:12:31 --> 00:12:35
Everybody, in history who has sinned

00:12:34 --> 00:12:37
is a person who's very different from me.

00:12:36 --> 00:12:39
Robert George, the philosopher over at Princeton,

00:12:38 --> 00:12:41
he's fond of doing a sort of thought experiment

00:12:40 --> 00:12:43
in his classes where he asks people

00:12:42 --> 00:12:45
to raise their hand if they had lived in Alabama

00:12:44 --> 00:12:48
in 1861, "How many of you would be abolitionists?"

00:12:47 --> 00:12:49
And everybody raises their hand.

00:12:48 --> 00:12:51
And he says, "Of course that's not true."

00:12:50 --> 00:12:53
Of course, that's not true.

00:12:52 --> 00:12:56
The best protection against evil is recognizing

00:12:55 --> 00:12:58
that it lies in every human heart,

00:12:57 --> 00:13:02
and the possibility that it takes you over.

00:13:01 --> 00:13:05
And so, you have to be very cautious in how you approach

00:13:04 --> 00:13:08
these issues, and the back and forth of politics,

00:13:07 --> 00:13:10
the sort of bipolarity of politics,

00:13:09 --> 00:13:12
or the polarization in politics

00:13:11 --> 00:13:14
might be a better way to put it,

00:13:13 --> 00:13:17
makes it very easy to kind of fall

00:13:16 --> 00:13:19
into the rock 'em sock 'em robots

00:13:18 --> 00:13:21
that eventually could theoretically allow you to support

00:13:20 --> 00:13:25
somebody who's truly frightening and hideous

00:13:24 --> 00:13:26
in order to stop somebody who you think

00:13:25 --> 00:13:28
is more frightening and hideous.

00:13:27 --> 00:13:29
And, you see this kinda language, by the way,

00:13:28 --> 00:13:32
now pre-dominating almost all over the western world, right.

00:13:31 --> 00:13:34
My political enemy is an enemy of democracy.

00:13:33 --> 00:13:36
My political enemy is gonna end the republic.

00:13:35 --> 00:13:37
My political enemy

00:13:36 --> 00:13:39
is going to be the person who destroys the country

00:13:38 --> 00:13:40
we live in.

00:13:39 --> 00:13:43
And so, that person has to be stopped

00:13:42 --> 00:13:45
by any means necessary.

00:13:44 --> 00:13:47
And, that's dangerous stuff.

00:13:46 --> 00:13:50
- So the communists had to be stopped in Nazi Germany,

00:13:49 --> 00:13:52
and so they're the devil.

00:13:51 --> 00:13:55
And so any useful buffoon,

00:13:54 --> 00:13:56
as long as they're effective

00:13:55 --> 00:13:59
against the communists would do.

00:13:58 --> 00:14:00
Do you ever wonder,

00:13:59 --> 00:14:02
because the people that are participating in evil

00:14:01 --> 00:14:05
may not understand that they're doing evil.

00:14:04 --> 00:14:07
Do you ever sit back, you know,

00:14:06 --> 00:14:09
in the quiet of your mind and think,

00:14:08 --> 00:14:11
Am I participating in evil?

00:14:10 --> 00:14:13
- I mean, my business partner and I,

00:14:12 --> 00:14:15
one of our favorite memes

00:14:14 --> 00:14:18
is from there's a British comedy show,

00:14:17 --> 00:14:19
the name escapes me.

00:14:18 --> 00:14:22
Of these two guys who are members of the SS,

00:14:21 --> 00:14:24
and they're dressed in the SS uniforms

00:14:23 --> 00:14:26
and the black uniforms with the skulls on them.

00:14:25 --> 00:14:28
And they're saying to each other, one says to the other guy,

00:14:27 --> 00:14:31
"You notice like the British, their symbol is something,

00:14:30 --> 00:14:32
"It's something nice.

00:14:31 --> 00:14:34
"And it's like, it's like an eagle,

00:14:33 --> 00:14:35
"But ours, it's a skull and crossbones.

00:14:34 --> 00:14:37
"You see that the Americans,

00:14:36 --> 00:14:38
"You see the, the blue uniforms very nice and pretty.

00:14:37 --> 00:14:42
"Ours are jet black, are we the baddies?"

00:14:41 --> 00:14:43
And you know, that's it.

00:14:42 --> 00:14:45
(hearty laughing)

00:14:44 --> 00:14:47
And the truth is, we look back at the Nazis and we say,

00:14:46 --> 00:14:48
"Well, of course they were the baddies.

00:14:47 --> 00:14:50
"They wore black uniforms and they had jack boots

00:14:49 --> 00:14:51
"And they had this..."

00:14:50 --> 00:14:53
And, of course they were the bad guys.

00:14:52 --> 00:14:56
But, evil rarely presents its face so clearly.

00:14:55 --> 00:14:57
So yeah, I mean,

00:14:56 --> 00:15:00
I think you have to constantly be thinking along those lines

00:14:59 --> 00:15:03
and hopefully you try to avoid it.

00:15:02 --> 00:15:05
You can only do the best that a human being can do.

00:15:04 --> 00:15:07
But yeah, the answer is yes,

00:15:07 --> 00:15:12
I would say that I spend an inordinate amount of time

00:15:11 --> 00:15:16
reflecting on whether I'm doing the right thing,

00:15:15 --> 00:15:17
and I may not always do the right thing.

00:15:16 --> 00:15:19
I'm sure a lot of people think that I'm doing the wrong

00:15:18 --> 00:15:20
thing on a daily basis.

00:15:19 --> 00:15:21
But, it's definitely a question

00:15:20 --> 00:15:23
that has to enter your mind,

00:15:22 --> 00:15:28
as a historically aware and hopefully morally decent person.

00:15:27 --> 00:15:28
- Do you think

00:15:27 --> 00:15:31
you're mentally strong enough, if you realize,

00:15:30 --> 00:15:35
that you are on the wrong side of history to switch sides?

00:15:34 --> 00:15:38
Very few people in history seem to be strong enough

00:15:37 --> 00:15:38
to do that.

00:15:37 --> 00:15:42
- I think that the answer, I hope would be yes.

00:15:41 --> 00:15:44
You never know until the time comes and you have to do it.

00:15:43 --> 00:15:48
I will say that having heterodox opinions in a wide variety

00:15:47 --> 00:15:52
of areas is something that I have done before.

00:15:51 --> 00:15:56
I'm the only person I've ever heard of in public life,

00:15:55 --> 00:15:58
who actually has a list on their website

00:15:57 --> 00:16:01
of all the dumb stupid things I've ever said.

00:16:00 --> 00:16:04
Where I go through and I either say,

00:16:03 --> 00:16:05
this is why I still believe this,

00:16:04 --> 00:16:07
or this is why what I said was terrible and stupid.

00:16:06 --> 00:16:09
- Yeah. - And I'm sure that list

00:16:08 --> 00:16:10
will get a lot longer.

00:16:09 --> 00:16:12
- Yeah, look forward to new additions to that.

00:16:11 --> 00:16:13
- [Ben] Yeah, exactly.

00:16:12 --> 00:16:16
- It actually is a super, super long list.

00:16:15 --> 00:16:17
People should check it out.

00:16:16 --> 00:16:18
And, it's quite honest and raw.

00:16:18 --> 00:16:21
What do you think about,

00:16:20 --> 00:16:22
it's interesting to ask you,

00:16:21 --> 00:16:26
given how pro-life you are,

00:16:25 --> 00:16:27
about Ye's comments,

00:16:26 --> 00:16:32
about comparing the Holocaust to the 900,000 abortions

00:16:32 --> 00:16:34
in the United States a year.

00:16:35 --> 00:16:36
- I'll take this from two angles.

00:16:35 --> 00:16:37
As a pro-life person,

00:16:36 --> 00:16:39
I actually didn't find it offensive

00:16:38 --> 00:16:41
because if you believe as I do,

00:16:40 --> 00:16:45
that unborn and pre-born lives deserve protection,

00:16:44 --> 00:16:48
then the slaughter of just under a million of them

00:16:47 --> 00:16:50
every year for the last almost 50 years,

00:16:49 --> 00:16:54
is a historic tragedy on par with a holocaust.

00:16:53 --> 00:16:55
From the outside perspective,

00:16:54 --> 00:16:57
I get why people would say there's a difference

00:16:56 --> 00:17:00
in how people view the pre-born as to how people view,

00:16:59 --> 00:17:02
say a seven year old, who's being killed in the Holocaust.

00:17:01 --> 00:17:05
The visceral power and evil of the Nazis

00:17:04 --> 00:17:07
hoving full grown human beings

00:17:06 --> 00:17:09
and small children into gas chambers,

00:17:08 --> 00:17:12
can't be compared to a person who,

00:17:11 --> 00:17:13
even from pro-life perspective,

00:17:12 --> 00:17:15
may not fully understand the consequences

00:17:14 --> 00:17:17
of their own decisions or from a pro-choice perspective,

00:17:16 --> 00:17:18
fully understands the consequences,

00:17:17 --> 00:17:20
but just doesn't think that that person is a person.

00:17:19 --> 00:17:22
That that's actually different.

00:17:21 --> 00:17:24
I understand both sides of it.

00:17:23 --> 00:17:27
I wasn't offended by Ye's comments in that way though,

00:17:26 --> 00:17:29
because if you're a pro-life human being,

00:17:28 --> 00:17:31
then you do think that what's happening

00:17:30 --> 00:17:33
is a great tragedy on scale that involves the dehumanization

00:17:32 --> 00:17:37
of an entire class of people, the pre-born.

00:17:36 --> 00:17:40
- So the philosophical, you understand the comparison?

00:17:39 --> 00:17:41
- I do, sure.

00:17:41 --> 00:17:44
- So in his comments,

00:17:43 --> 00:17:47
in the jumping from the individual to the group,

00:17:46 --> 00:17:48
I'd like to ask you,

00:17:47 --> 00:17:50
you're one of the most effective people in the world

00:17:49 --> 00:17:51
that attacking the left,

00:17:51 --> 00:17:55
and sometimes it can slip into attacking the group.

00:17:56 --> 00:17:59
Do you worry that that's the same kind

00:17:58 --> 00:18:01
of oversimplification that Ye's.

00:18:00 --> 00:18:02
doing about Jewish people?

00:18:01 --> 00:18:06
That you can sometimes do with the left as a group?

00:18:05 --> 00:18:07
- So when I speak about the left,

00:18:06 --> 00:18:10
I'm speaking about a philosophy.

00:18:09 --> 00:18:12
I'm not really speaking about individual human beings

00:18:11 --> 00:18:15
as the leftist group,

00:18:14 --> 00:18:17
and then try to name who the members of this

00:18:16 --> 00:18:17
individual group are.

00:18:16 --> 00:18:20
I also make a distinction between the left and liberals.

00:18:19 --> 00:18:22
There are a lot of people who are liberal who disagree

00:18:21 --> 00:18:25
with me on taxes, disagree with me on foreign policy,

00:18:24 --> 00:18:26
disagree with me on a lot of things.

00:18:25 --> 00:18:27
The people who I'm talking about generally,

00:18:26 --> 00:18:30
and I talk about the left in the United States;

00:18:29 --> 00:18:32
are people who believe that alternative points of view

00:18:31 --> 00:18:33
ought to be silenced

00:18:32 --> 00:18:36
because they are damaging and harmful

00:18:35 --> 00:18:37
simply based on the disagreement.

00:18:36 --> 00:18:39
So that's one distinction.

00:18:38 --> 00:18:40
The second distinction,

00:18:39 --> 00:18:41
again, is when I talk about the right versus the left.

00:18:40 --> 00:18:43
Typically, I'm talking about a battle

00:18:42 --> 00:18:45
of competing philosophies.

00:18:44 --> 00:18:46
And so, I'm not speaking about,

00:18:45 --> 00:18:49
typically, it would be hard to,

00:18:48 --> 00:18:50
if you put a person in front of me and said,

00:18:49 --> 00:18:52
"Is this person of the left or of the right?"

00:18:51 --> 00:18:54
Having just met them, I wouldn't be able to label them

00:18:53 --> 00:18:56
in the same way that if you met somebody

00:18:55 --> 00:18:58
with the name of Greenstein, you immediately got Jew.

00:18:57 --> 00:19:00
Or you met a black person, black person.

00:18:59 --> 00:19:03
And, the adherence to a philosophy

00:19:02 --> 00:19:06
makes you a member of a group.

00:19:05 --> 00:19:07
If I think the philosophy is bad,

00:19:06 --> 00:19:10
that doesn't necessarily mean that you as a person are bad,

00:19:09 --> 00:19:13
but it does mean that I think your philosophy is bad.

00:19:12 --> 00:19:13
- Yeah, so the grouping

00:19:12 --> 00:19:15
is based on the philosophy versus something

00:19:15 --> 00:19:19
like a race, like the color of your skin,

00:19:18 --> 00:19:21
or race, in the case of the Jewish people.

00:19:20 --> 00:19:23
So it's a different thing.

00:19:22 --> 00:19:27
You, can be a little bit more nonchalant and careless

00:19:26 --> 00:19:28
in attacking a group

00:19:27 --> 00:19:30
because it's ultimately attacking a set of ideas.

00:19:29 --> 00:19:32
- Well, I mean, it is really nonchalant

00:19:31 --> 00:19:33
in attacking the set of ideas.

00:19:32 --> 00:19:34
And, I don't know that nonchalant would be the way

00:19:33 --> 00:19:36
I'd put it.

00:19:35 --> 00:19:37
I try to be exact.

00:19:36 --> 00:19:38
When you don't always hit,

00:19:37 --> 00:19:42
but you know, if I say that, I oppose the communists,

00:19:41 --> 00:19:46
And then, presumably I'm speaking of people who believe

00:19:45 --> 00:19:48
in the communists philosophy.

00:19:47 --> 00:19:50
Now, the question is whether I'm mislabeling.

00:19:49 --> 00:19:51
Whether I'm taking somebody who's not actually a communist

00:19:50 --> 00:19:53
and then shoving them in that group of communists.

00:19:52 --> 00:19:54
That would be inaccurate.

00:19:53 --> 00:19:58
- The dangerous thing is it expands the group as opposed

00:19:57 --> 00:20:00
to you talking about the philosophy.

00:19:59 --> 00:20:02
You're throwing everybody who's ever said,

00:20:01 --> 00:20:06
"I'm curious about communism, I'm curious about socialism."

00:20:05 --> 00:20:08
'Cause there's like a gradient,

00:20:07 --> 00:20:11
it's like to throw something at you.

00:20:10 --> 00:20:13
I think Joe Biden said, "MAGA Republicans."

00:20:12 --> 00:20:14
- Right. - Right.

00:20:15 --> 00:20:17
I think that's a very careless statement

00:20:16 --> 00:20:21
because the thing you jump to immediately is like all-

00:20:20 --> 00:20:23
Vote for Trump. - All Republicans, right.

00:20:22 --> 00:20:25
- Right. - Versus, I think,

00:20:24 --> 00:20:27
in the charitable interpretation,

00:20:26 --> 00:20:29
that means a set of ideas.

00:20:28 --> 00:20:30
- Yeah, my actually problem

00:20:30 --> 00:20:32
with the MAGA Republicans line

00:20:31 --> 00:20:35
from Biden is that he went on in the speech that he made

00:20:34 --> 00:20:36
in front of Independence Hall

00:20:35 --> 00:20:38
to actually trying and find what it meant

00:20:37 --> 00:20:40
o be a MAGA Republican,

00:20:39 --> 00:20:41
who was a threat to the republic,

00:20:40 --> 00:20:42
was the kinda language that he was using.

00:20:41 --> 00:20:45
And later on in the speech he actually suggested, well,

00:20:44 --> 00:20:46
there are moderate Republicans.

00:20:45 --> 00:20:48
And, the moderate Republicans are people who agree with me

00:20:47 --> 00:20:49
on like the Inflation Reduction Act.

00:20:48 --> 00:20:52
It's like, well that can't be the dividing line

00:20:51 --> 00:20:55
between a MAGA Republican and a moderate.

00:20:54 --> 00:20:56
Like a moderate Republican

00:20:55 --> 00:20:57
is somebody who agrees with you,

00:20:56 --> 00:20:58
that you gotta name me,

00:20:57 --> 00:21:01
like a republican who disagrees with you fairly strenuously,

00:21:00 --> 00:21:04
but is not in this group of threats to the republic.

00:21:03 --> 00:21:05
You make that distinction,

00:21:04 --> 00:21:06
we can have a fair discussion

00:21:05 --> 00:21:08
about whether the idea of election denial,

00:21:07 --> 00:21:12
for example, makes somebody a threat to institutions.

00:21:11 --> 00:21:14
That's a conversation that we can have.

00:21:13 --> 00:21:16
And then, we'll have to discuss how much power they have,

00:21:15 --> 00:21:18
what the actual perspective is, delve into it.

00:21:17 --> 00:21:19
But you know,

00:21:18 --> 00:21:21
I think that he was being over broad and sort of labeling

00:21:20 --> 00:21:23
all of his political enemies under one rubric.

00:21:22 --> 00:21:24
Now again, in politics,

00:21:23 --> 00:21:25
this stuff sort of happens all the time.

00:21:24 --> 00:21:27
I'm not gonna plead clean hands here,

00:21:26 --> 00:21:29
'cause I'm sure that I've been inexact.

00:21:28 --> 00:21:32
But, what would be good

00:21:31 --> 00:21:34
in that particular situation is for somebody

00:21:33 --> 00:21:35
to sort of read me back the quote,

00:21:34 --> 00:21:37
and I'll let you know where I've been inaccurate.

00:21:36 --> 00:21:38
I'll try to do that.

00:21:37 --> 00:21:40
- And also, you don't shy away from humor

00:21:39 --> 00:21:42
and occasional trolling and mockery,

00:21:41 --> 00:21:43
and all that kind of stuff

00:21:42 --> 00:21:45
or the fun, for the chaos, all that kind of stuff.

00:21:44 --> 00:21:49
- I mean, I try not to to do trollery for trollery sake,

00:21:48 --> 00:21:51
but, if the show's not entertaining and not fun,

00:21:50 --> 00:21:53
people aren't gonna listen.

00:21:52 --> 00:21:55
And so, if you can't have fun with politics,

00:21:54 --> 00:21:57
the truth about politics is we all take it very seriously.

00:21:56 --> 00:21:59
'Cause it has some serious ramifications.

00:21:58 --> 00:21:59
Politics is Veep,

00:21:58 --> 00:22:01
it is not House of Cards.

00:22:00 --> 00:22:05
The general rule of politics is that everyone is a moron

00:22:04 --> 00:22:06
unless proven otherwise.

00:22:05 --> 00:22:08
That virtually everything is done out of stupidity

00:22:07 --> 00:22:09
rather than malice.

00:22:08 --> 00:22:12
And that, if you actually watch politics as a comedy,

00:22:11 --> 00:22:13
you'll have a lot more fun.

00:22:12 --> 00:22:15
And so, the difficulty for me is I take politics seriously,

00:22:14 --> 00:22:17
but also I have the ability to sort of flip the switch

00:22:16 --> 00:22:19
and suddenly it all becomes incredibly funny

00:22:18 --> 00:22:21
because it really is.

00:22:20 --> 00:22:24
If you just watch it from a pure entertainment perspective

00:22:23 --> 00:22:25
and you put aside the fact that it affects

00:22:24 --> 00:22:26
hundreds of millions of people,

00:22:25 --> 00:22:31
then watching President Trump,

00:22:30 --> 00:22:33
I mean, he's one of the funniest humans

00:22:32 --> 00:22:34
who's ever lived watching.

00:22:33 --> 00:22:36
Watching Kamala Harris be Kamala Harris

00:22:35 --> 00:22:38
and talking about how much she loves Venn diagrams

00:22:37 --> 00:22:39
or electric buses.

00:22:38 --> 00:22:41
I mean, that's funny stuff.

00:22:40 --> 00:22:42
So if I can't make fun of that,

00:22:41 --> 00:22:44
then my job becomes pretty morose pretty quickly.

00:22:43 --> 00:22:46
- Yeah, it's funny to figure out

00:22:45 --> 00:22:50
what is the perfect balance between seeing the humor

00:22:49 --> 00:22:52
and the absurdity of the game of it,

00:22:51 --> 00:22:55
versus taking it seriously enough

00:22:54 --> 00:22:57
because it does affect hundreds of millions of people.

00:22:56 --> 00:22:59
It's a weird balance to strike.

00:22:58 --> 00:23:02
It's like, I am afraid with the internet that everything

00:23:01 --> 00:23:04
becomes a joke.

00:23:03 --> 00:23:05
- I totally agree with this.

00:23:04 --> 00:23:06
I will say this,

00:23:05 --> 00:23:09
I try to make less jokes about the ideas

00:23:08 --> 00:23:10
and more jokes about the people in the same way

00:23:09 --> 00:23:12
that I make jokes about myself.

00:23:11 --> 00:23:14
I'm pretty self-effacing in terms of my humor.

00:23:13 --> 00:23:18
I would say at least half the jokes on my show are about me.

00:23:17 --> 00:23:19
When I'm reading ads Tommy John

00:23:18 --> 00:23:21
and they're talking about their no wedgie guarantee,

00:23:20 --> 00:23:22
I'll say things like,

00:23:21 --> 00:23:25
"That would help me in high school, because it would've."

00:23:24 --> 00:23:27
I mean, just factually speaking,

00:23:26 --> 00:23:29
if I can speak that way about myself,

00:23:28 --> 00:23:32
I feel like everybody else can take it as well.

00:23:31 --> 00:23:33
- Difficult question.

00:23:32 --> 00:23:37
In 2017, there was a mosque shooting in Quebec City.

00:23:36 --> 00:23:40
Six people died, five others seriously injured.

00:23:39 --> 00:23:44
The 27 year old gunman consumed a lot of content online

00:23:43 --> 00:23:47
and checked Twitter accounts of a lot of people.

00:23:46 --> 00:23:51
But, one of the people he checked quite a lot of is you,

00:23:50 --> 00:23:54
93 times in the month leading up to the shooting.

00:23:53 --> 00:23:56
If you could talk to that young man,

00:23:55 --> 00:23:59
what would you tell him and maybe other young men listening

00:23:58 --> 00:24:03
to this that have hate in their heart in that same way,

00:24:02 --> 00:24:04
What would you tell?

00:24:03 --> 00:24:06
- "You're getting it wrong.

00:24:05 --> 00:24:09
"If anything that I or anyone else in mainstream politics

00:24:08 --> 00:24:12
"Says drives you to violence, you're getting it wrong.

00:24:11 --> 00:24:14
"You're getting it wrong."

00:24:13 --> 00:24:16
Now again, when it comes to stuff like this,

00:24:15 --> 00:24:18
I have a hard and fast rule that I've applied evenly

00:24:17 --> 00:24:19
across the spectrum.

00:24:18 --> 00:24:20
And that is,

00:24:19 --> 00:24:23
I never blame people's politics for other people

00:24:22 --> 00:24:24
committing acts of violence;

00:24:23 --> 00:24:26
unless they're actively advocating violence.

00:24:25 --> 00:24:29
So when a fan of Bernie Sanders shoots up a congressional

00:24:28 --> 00:24:31
baseball game, that is not Bernie Sanders's fault.

00:24:30 --> 00:24:32
I may not like his rhetoric.

00:24:31 --> 00:24:33
I may disagree with him on everything.

00:24:32 --> 00:24:35
Bernie Sanders did not tell somebody to go shoot up

00:24:34 --> 00:24:36
a congressional baseball game.

00:24:35 --> 00:24:39
When a nut case in San Francisco

00:24:38 --> 00:24:41
goes and hits Paul Pelosi with a hammer.

00:24:40 --> 00:24:43
I'm not gonna blame Kevin McCarthy,

00:24:42 --> 00:24:44
the house speaker, for that.

00:24:43 --> 00:24:46
When somebody threatens Brett Kavanaugh,

00:24:45 --> 00:24:49
I'm not gonna suggest that that was Joe Biden's fault

00:24:48 --> 00:24:50
because it's not Joe Biden's fault.

00:24:49 --> 00:24:52
We can play this game all day long.

00:24:51 --> 00:24:55
And, I find that the people who are most intensely focused

00:24:54 --> 00:24:58
on playing this game are people who tend to oppose

00:24:57 --> 00:24:59
the politics of the person,

00:24:58 --> 00:25:02
as opposed to actually believing sincerely

00:25:01 --> 00:25:04
that this has driven somebody into the arms

00:25:03 --> 00:25:06
of the god of violence.

00:25:05 --> 00:25:10
But, I have 4.7 million Twitter followers,

00:25:09 --> 00:25:12
I have 8 million Facebook followers,

00:25:11 --> 00:25:14
I have 5 million YouTube followers.

00:25:13 --> 00:25:16
I would imagine that some of them

00:25:15 --> 00:25:19
are people who are violent.

00:25:18 --> 00:25:20
I would imagine that some of them are people who do evil

00:25:19 --> 00:25:23
things or want to do evil things.

00:25:22 --> 00:25:26
And, I wish that there were a wand that we could wave

00:25:25 --> 00:25:28
that would prevent those people from deliberately

00:25:27 --> 00:25:32
or mistakenly misinterpreting things as a call of violence.

00:25:31 --> 00:25:34
It's just a negative byproduct of the fact

00:25:33 --> 00:25:35
hat you can reach a lot of people.

00:25:34 --> 00:25:39
And so, if somebody could point me to the comment

00:25:39 --> 00:25:43
hat I suppose quote unquote drove somebody to going

00:25:42 --> 00:25:46
literally murder human beings, then I would appreciate it.

00:25:45 --> 00:25:50
So I could talk about the comment.

00:25:49 --> 00:25:53
But, I don't, mainly because I just think

00:25:52 --> 00:25:57
that if we remove agency from individuals,

00:25:56 --> 00:26:01
and if we blame broad scale political rhetoric

00:26:00 --> 00:26:02
for every act of violence, we're not gonna...

00:26:01 --> 00:26:05
The people who are gonna pay the price are actually

00:26:04 --> 00:26:08
the general population because free speech will go away.

00:26:07 --> 00:26:11
If the idea is that things that we say could drive somebody,

00:26:10 --> 00:26:14
who is unbalanced, to go do something evil,

00:26:13 --> 00:26:16
the necessary byproduct is hate.

00:26:15 --> 00:26:19
Is that, speech is a form of hate.

00:26:18 --> 00:26:21
Hate is a form of violence, speech is a form of violence.

00:26:21 --> 00:26:23
Speech needs to be curbed.

00:26:22 --> 00:26:26
And that. to me is deeply disturbing.

00:26:25 --> 00:26:30
- So definitely, that man,

00:26:29 --> 00:26:31
that 27 year old man,

00:26:30 --> 00:26:34
is the only one responsible for the evil he did.

00:26:33 --> 00:26:37
But, what if he and others like him are not nut cases?

00:26:38 --> 00:26:43
What if they're people with pain, with anger in their heart?

00:26:42 --> 00:26:45
What would you say to them?

00:26:44 --> 00:26:49
You're exceptionally influential and other people like you,

00:26:48 --> 00:26:52
that speak passionately about ideas.

00:26:51 --> 00:26:55
What do you think is your opportunity to alleviate the hate

00:26:54 --> 00:26:56
in their heart?

00:26:55 --> 00:26:58
- If we're speaking about people who aren't mentally ill

00:26:57 --> 00:27:01
and people who are just misguided,

00:27:00 --> 00:27:04
I'd say to him, the thing I said to every other young man

00:27:03 --> 00:27:05
in the country,

00:27:04 --> 00:27:08
you need to find meaning and purpose in forming connections

00:27:07 --> 00:27:09
that actually matter,

00:27:09 --> 00:27:14
in a belief system that actually promotes general prosperity

00:27:15 --> 00:27:17
and promotes helping other people.

00:27:16 --> 00:27:19
And this is why,

00:27:18 --> 00:27:21
the message that I most commonly say to young men is,

00:27:20 --> 00:27:24
it's time for you to grow up, mature, get a job,

00:27:23 --> 00:27:25
get married, have a family,

00:27:24 --> 00:27:26
take care of the people around you,

00:27:25 --> 00:27:28
become a useful part of your community.

00:27:27 --> 00:27:32
I've never at any point in my entire career suggested

00:27:31 --> 00:27:37
violence as a resort to political issues.

00:27:37 --> 00:27:40
The whole point of having a political conversation

00:27:39 --> 00:27:41
is that it's a conversation.

00:27:40 --> 00:27:43
If I didn't think that it were worth trying to convince

00:27:42 --> 00:27:44
people of my point of view,

00:27:43 --> 00:27:46
I wouldn't do what I do for a living.

00:27:45 --> 00:27:48
- So violence doesn't solve anything?

00:27:47 --> 00:27:49
- No, it doesn't.

00:27:49 --> 00:27:54
- As if this wasn't already a difficult conversation.

00:27:55 --> 00:27:59
Let me ask about Ilhan Omar.

00:27:58 --> 00:28:01
You've called out her criticism

00:28:00 --> 00:28:04
of Israel policies as antisemitic.

00:28:03 --> 00:28:07
Is there a difference between criticizing a race of people,

00:28:06 --> 00:28:10
like the Jews, and criticizing

00:28:10 --> 00:28:13
the policies of a nation like Israel?

00:28:12 --> 00:28:14
- Of course, of course.

00:28:13 --> 00:28:15
I criticize the policies of Israel

00:28:14 --> 00:28:16
on a fairly regular basis.

00:28:15 --> 00:28:19
I would assume from a different angle than Ilhan Omar does.

00:28:18 --> 00:28:21
But yeah, I mean, I criticize the policies

00:28:20 --> 00:28:23
of a wide variety of states.

00:28:22 --> 00:28:24
And to take an example,

00:28:23 --> 00:28:27
I've criticized Israel's policy in giving control

00:28:26 --> 00:28:29
of the Temple Mount to the Islamic Waqf;

00:28:28 --> 00:28:31
which effectively prevents anybody except for Muslims

00:28:30 --> 00:28:32
from praying up there.

00:28:31 --> 00:28:34
I've also criticized the Israeli government

00:28:33 --> 00:28:35
for their COVID crackdown.

00:28:34 --> 00:28:37
They can criticize the policies of any government,

00:28:36 --> 00:28:38
but that's not what Ilhan Omar does.

00:28:37 --> 00:28:40
Ilhan Omar doesn't actually believe

00:28:39 --> 00:28:41
that there should be a state of Israel.

00:28:40 --> 00:28:43
She believes that Zionism is racism

00:28:42 --> 00:28:46
and that the existence of a Jewish state in Israel,

00:28:45 --> 00:28:49
is in and of itself the great sin.

00:28:48 --> 00:28:51
That is a statement she would make about no other people

00:28:50 --> 00:28:53
in no other land.

00:28:52 --> 00:28:54
She would not say that the French don't deserve a state

00:28:53 --> 00:28:55
for the French.

00:28:54 --> 00:28:57
She wouldn't say that Somalis wouldn't deserve

00:28:56 --> 00:28:58
a state in Somalia.

00:28:57 --> 00:29:00
She wouldn't say that that Germans

00:28:59 --> 00:29:01
don't deserve a state in Germany.

00:29:00 --> 00:29:04
She wouldn't say for the 50 plus Islamic states

00:29:03 --> 00:29:05
that exist across the world,

00:29:04 --> 00:29:07
that they don't deserve states of their own.

00:29:06 --> 00:29:08
It is only the Jewish state

00:29:07 --> 00:29:10
that has fallen under her significant scrutiny.

00:29:09 --> 00:29:15
And she also promulgates laws about one specific state

00:29:14 --> 00:29:17
in the form of suggesting, for example,

00:29:16 --> 00:29:18
that Israel is an apartheid state;

00:29:17 --> 00:29:21
which it is most eminently not considering that

00:29:20 --> 00:29:23
he last unity government in Israel included an Arab party.

00:29:22 --> 00:29:24
That there are Arabs who sit

00:29:23 --> 00:29:26
on the Israeli Supreme Court and all the rest.

00:29:25 --> 00:29:27
And then, beyond that,

00:29:26 --> 00:29:29
obviously she's engaged in some of the same sort

00:29:28 --> 00:29:30
of antisemitic ropes that you heard from Ye, right.

00:29:29 --> 00:29:32
The stuff about,

00:29:31 --> 00:29:33
it's all about the Benjamins,

00:29:32 --> 00:29:35
that American support for Israel is all about the Benjamins.

00:29:34 --> 00:29:37
And, she's had to be chided by members of her own party

00:29:36 --> 00:29:39
about this sort of stuff before.

00:29:38 --> 00:29:42
- Can you empathize with the plight of Palestinian people?

00:29:41 --> 00:29:43
- Absolutely.

00:29:43 --> 00:29:46
Some of the uglier things that I've ever said in my career

00:29:45 --> 00:29:48
are things that I said very early on, when I was 17, 18, 19.

00:29:47 --> 00:29:50
I started writing a syndicated comment, I was 17.

00:29:49 --> 00:29:51
I'm now 38.

00:29:50 --> 00:29:52
So virtually all the dumb things,

00:29:51 --> 00:29:54
I'd not say virtually all, many of the dumb things,

00:29:53 --> 00:29:56
the plurality of the dumb things that I've said came from

00:29:55 --> 00:29:59
the ages of, I would say 17 to maybe 23.

00:29:58 --> 00:30:00
- Yeah.

00:29:59 --> 00:30:02
- And they are rooted, again, in sloppy thinking.

00:30:01 --> 00:30:06
I feel terrible for people who have lived under the thumb

00:30:05 --> 00:30:08
and currently live under the thumb of Hamas;

00:30:07 --> 00:30:10
which is a national terrorist group,

00:30:09 --> 00:30:11
or the Palestinian authority;

00:30:10 --> 00:30:13
which is a corrupt oligarchy that steals money

00:30:12 --> 00:30:16
from its people and leaves them in misery.

00:30:15 --> 00:30:17
Or Islamic Jihad,

00:30:16 --> 00:30:19
which is an actual terrorist group.

00:30:18 --> 00:30:24
The basic rule for the region in my view, is if these groups

00:30:23 --> 00:30:26
were willing to make peace with Israel,

00:30:25 --> 00:30:28
they would have a state literally tomorrow.

00:30:27 --> 00:30:30
And if they are not, then there will be no peace.

00:30:29 --> 00:30:31
And, it really is that simple.

00:30:30 --> 00:30:34
If Israel, the formulas typically used,

00:30:33 --> 00:30:36
it's become a bit of a bumper sticker,

00:30:35 --> 00:30:37
but it happens to be factually correct.

00:30:36 --> 00:30:39
If the Palestinians put down their guns tomorrow,

00:30:38 --> 00:30:40
there would be a state.

00:30:39 --> 00:30:41
If the Israelis put down their guns,

00:30:40 --> 00:30:42
there'd be no Israel.

00:30:45 --> 00:30:48
- You get attacked a lot on the internet.

00:30:47 --> 00:30:50
- Oh yeah, you noticed.

00:30:49 --> 00:30:52
- I gotta ask you about your own psychology.

00:30:52 --> 00:30:56
How do you not let that break you mentally?

00:30:56 --> 00:31:01
And, how do you avoid letting that lead to a resentment

00:31:00 --> 00:31:03
of the groups that attack you?

00:31:03 --> 00:31:05
- There are a few sort of practical things

00:31:04 --> 00:31:06
that I've done.

00:31:05 --> 00:31:08
For example, I would say that four years ago,

00:31:07 --> 00:31:10
Twitter was all consuming.

00:31:09 --> 00:31:11
Twitter is an ego machine,

00:31:10 --> 00:31:13
especially the notifications button, right.

00:31:12 --> 00:31:15
The notifications button is just people talking about you

00:31:14 --> 00:31:16
all the time.

00:31:15 --> 00:31:17
And the normal human tendency as,

00:31:16 --> 00:31:18
"Wow, people talking about me,

00:31:17 --> 00:31:19
"I gotta see what they're saying about me."

00:31:18 --> 00:31:21
Which is a recipe for insanity.

00:31:20 --> 00:31:23
So my wife actually said,

00:31:22 --> 00:31:24
"Twitter is making your life miserable.

00:31:23 --> 00:31:25
"You need to take it off your phone."

00:31:24 --> 00:31:27
So Twitter is not on my phone.

00:31:26 --> 00:31:28
If I wanna log onto Twitter,

00:31:27 --> 00:31:30
I have to go onto my computer

00:31:29 --> 00:31:32
and I have to make the conscious decision

00:31:31 --> 00:31:34
to go onto Twitter and then take a look

00:31:33 --> 00:31:34
at what's going on.

00:31:33 --> 00:31:35
- I could just imagine you like,

00:31:34 --> 00:31:37
there's a computer in the basement,

00:31:36 --> 00:31:39
you dissent into the check Twitter.

00:31:38 --> 00:31:40
- That's pretty much-

00:31:39 --> 00:31:41
In the darkness.

00:31:40 --> 00:31:41
- If you look at, when I actually tweet,

00:31:40 --> 00:31:44
it's generally in the run up to recording my show

00:31:43 --> 00:31:46
or when I'm prepping for my show,

00:31:45 --> 00:31:48
later in the afternoon, for example.

00:31:47 --> 00:31:50
- That doesn't affect you negatively mentally?

00:31:49 --> 00:31:51
Like puts you in a bad mental space?

00:31:50 --> 00:31:53
- Not particularly if it's restricted,

00:31:52 --> 00:31:55
to sort of what's being watched now.

00:31:54 --> 00:31:57
I will say that I think the most important thing

00:31:56 --> 00:32:01
is you have to surround yourself with a group of people

00:32:00 --> 00:32:04
who you trust enough to make serious critiques of you,

00:32:03 --> 00:32:05
when you're doing something wrong.

00:32:04 --> 00:32:06
But also, you know that they have

00:32:05 --> 00:32:08
your best interests at heart.

00:32:07 --> 00:32:09
'Cause the internet is filled with people

00:32:08 --> 00:32:10
who don't have your best interests at heart,

00:32:09 --> 00:32:11
and who hate your guts.

00:32:10 --> 00:32:13
And so, you can't really take those critiques seriously

00:32:12 --> 00:32:15
or it does wreck you.

00:32:14 --> 00:32:17
And the world is also filled with (indistinct), right.

00:32:17 --> 00:32:19
The, the, the more successful you become,

00:32:18 --> 00:32:21
there are a lot of people who will tell you,

00:32:20 --> 00:32:22
"You're always doing the right thing."

00:32:21 --> 00:32:23
I'm very lucky.

00:32:22 --> 00:32:25
I got married when I was 24, my wife was 20,

00:32:24 --> 00:32:27
so she's known me long before I was famous

00:32:26 --> 00:32:28
or wealthy or anything.

00:32:27 --> 00:32:31
And so, she's a good sounding board.

00:32:30 --> 00:32:33
I have a family that's willing to call me out

00:32:32 --> 00:32:36
on my bullshit, as you talk Ye about.

00:32:35 --> 00:32:39
I have friends who are able to do that.

00:32:38 --> 00:32:41
I try to have open lines of communications

00:32:40 --> 00:32:43
with people who I believe have my best interest at heart.

00:32:42 --> 00:32:46
But, one of the sort of conditions of being friends,

00:32:45 --> 00:32:47
is that when you see me do something wrong,

00:32:46 --> 00:32:50
I'd like for you to let me know that, so I can correct it.

00:32:49 --> 00:32:52
And, I don't wanna leave bad impressions out.

00:32:51 --> 00:32:53
- The sad thing about the internet,

00:32:52 --> 00:32:55
just looking at the critiques you get,

00:32:54 --> 00:32:58
I see very few critiques from people that actually want you

00:32:57 --> 00:32:59
to succeed, want you to grow.

00:32:58 --> 00:33:02
I mean, they're very, they're not sophisticated.

00:33:01 --> 00:33:06
They're just, I don't know, they're cruel.

00:33:05 --> 00:33:08
The critiques are just, it's not actual critiques,

00:33:07 --> 00:33:09
it's just cruelty.

00:33:08 --> 00:33:10
- And, that's most of Twitter.

00:33:09 --> 00:33:13
I mean, as I said, Twitter is a place

00:33:13 --> 00:33:15
to smack and be smacked.

00:33:15 --> 00:33:17
I mean, anybody who uses Twitter

00:33:16 --> 00:33:20
for an intellectual conversation,

00:33:19 --> 00:33:22
I think, is engaging in category error.

00:33:21 --> 00:33:23
- I use it to spread love.

00:33:22 --> 00:33:24
I think it's still possible.

00:33:23 --> 00:33:25
- Yeah, you're the only one.

00:33:24 --> 00:33:27
It's you, it's you and no one else, my friend.

00:33:26 --> 00:33:27
(hearty laughing)

00:33:26 --> 00:33:29
- Right, well, on that topic,

00:33:28 --> 00:33:30
what do you think about Elon buying Twitter?

00:33:30 --> 00:33:32
What do you like?

00:33:32 --> 00:33:35
What do you hope for on that front?

00:33:34 --> 00:33:37
What would you like to see Twitter improve?

00:33:36 --> 00:33:41
- So, I'm very hopeful about Elon buying Twitter.

00:33:40 --> 00:33:44
I think that Elon is significantly more transparent,

00:33:43 --> 00:33:47
than what has taken place up till now.

00:33:46 --> 00:33:49
He seems committed to the idea that he's gonna broaden

00:33:48 --> 00:33:51
the Overton Window to allow for conversations

00:33:50 --> 00:33:53
that simply were banned before.

00:33:52 --> 00:33:55
Everything ranging from efficacy of masks,

00:33:54 --> 00:33:56
with regard to COVID,

00:33:55 --> 00:33:59
to whether men can become women, and all the rest.

00:33:58 --> 00:34:01
A lot of things that would get you banned on Twitter

00:34:00 --> 00:34:03
before without any sort of real explanation.

00:34:02 --> 00:34:06
It seems like he's dedicated to at least explaining

00:34:05 --> 00:34:09
what the standards are going to be and being broader

00:34:08 --> 00:34:11
in allowing a variety of perspectives on the outlet.

00:34:10 --> 00:34:13
Which I think is wonderful.

00:34:12 --> 00:34:15
I think that's also why people are freaking out.

00:34:14 --> 00:34:16
I think the kind of wailing,

00:34:15 --> 00:34:19
and gnashing of teeth, and wearing of sack cloth and ash

00:34:18 --> 00:34:21
by so many members of the legacy media,

00:34:20 --> 00:34:24
I think a lot of that is because Twitter

00:34:23 --> 00:34:27
essentially was an oligarchy

00:34:26 --> 00:34:28
in which certain perspectives were allowed

00:34:27 --> 00:34:30
and certain perspectives just were not.

00:34:29 --> 00:34:34
And, that was part of a broader social media reimposed

00:34:33 --> 00:34:37
oligarchy in the aftermath of 2017.

00:34:36 --> 00:34:40
So in order for just to really understand, I think,

00:34:39 --> 00:34:43
what it means for Elon to take over Twitter,

00:34:42 --> 00:34:45
I think that we have to take a look at sort of the history

00:34:44 --> 00:34:48
of media in the United States in two minutes or less.

00:34:47 --> 00:34:49
The United States, the media,

00:34:48 --> 00:34:52
for most of its existence, up until about 1990,

00:34:51 --> 00:34:54
at least from about 1930s until the 1990s,

00:34:53 --> 00:34:57
virtually all media was three major television networks,

00:34:56 --> 00:34:59
a couple major newspapers and the wire services.

00:34:58 --> 00:35:01
Everybody had a local newspaper with wire services,

00:35:00 --> 00:35:03
that basically did all the foreign policy

00:35:02 --> 00:35:04
and all the national policy.

00:35:03 --> 00:35:06
McClatchy, Reuters, AP, AFP, et cetera.

00:35:06 --> 00:35:09
That monopoly oligopoly existed

00:35:08 --> 00:35:11
until the rise of the internet.

00:35:10 --> 00:35:14
There were sort of pokes at it; in talk radio and Fox News,

00:35:13 --> 00:35:16
but there certainly was not this plethora of sources.

00:35:15 --> 00:35:19
Then, the internet explodes and all of a sudden

00:35:18 --> 00:35:20
you can get news everywhere.

00:35:19 --> 00:35:22
And, the way that people are accessing that news,

00:35:22 --> 00:35:24
I believe, significantly younger than I am.

00:35:23 --> 00:35:26
But we used to do this thing called bookmarking,

00:35:25 --> 00:35:29
where you would bookmark a series of websites,

00:35:28 --> 00:35:31
and then you would visit them every morning.

00:35:30 --> 00:35:33
And then, social media came up.

00:35:32 --> 00:35:35
- Was this on AOL or?

00:35:34 --> 00:35:36
- Yeah, exactly.

00:35:35 --> 00:35:36
You had the dial up,

00:35:35 --> 00:35:38
it was actually a can connected to a string

00:35:37 --> 00:35:40
and you would actually, it would go.

00:35:39 --> 00:35:42
(high pitched squeaking)

00:35:41 --> 00:35:45
And, then there came a point where social media arose.

00:35:45 --> 00:35:48
And, social media was sort of a boon for everybody

00:35:47 --> 00:35:49
because you no longer had to bookmark anything.

00:35:48 --> 00:35:50
You just followed your favorite accounts,

00:35:49 --> 00:35:52
and all of them would pop up,

00:35:51 --> 00:35:53
and you followed everything on Facebook

00:35:52 --> 00:35:55
and it would all pop up and it was all centralized.

00:35:54 --> 00:35:56
And, for a while everybody was super happy

00:35:55 --> 00:35:58
because this was the brand new wave of the future,

00:35:57 --> 00:35:59
made everything super easy.

00:35:58 --> 00:36:03
Suddenly, outlets like mine were able to see new eyeballs

00:36:02 --> 00:36:04
because it was all centralized in one place, right.

00:36:03 --> 00:36:06
You didn't have to do it through Google optimization,

00:36:05 --> 00:36:08
you could now just put it on Facebook,

00:36:07 --> 00:36:09
and so many eyeballs were on Facebook,

00:36:08 --> 00:36:10
you'd get more traffic.

00:36:09 --> 00:36:12
And everybody seemed pretty happy with this arrangement,

00:36:11 --> 00:36:14
until precisely the moment Donald Trump became president.

00:36:13 --> 00:36:19
At that point then the sort of preexisting supposition

00:36:18 --> 00:36:21
of a lot of the powers that be;

00:36:20 --> 00:36:23
which was Democrats are gonna continue winning

00:36:22 --> 00:36:24
from here on out.

00:36:23 --> 00:36:26
So, we can sort of use these social media platforms

00:36:25 --> 00:36:29
as ways to push our information

00:36:28 --> 00:36:30
and still allow for there

00:36:29 --> 00:36:32
to be other information out there.

00:36:31 --> 00:36:35
The immediate response was, we need to reestablish

00:36:34 --> 00:36:37
this siphoning of information.

00:36:36 --> 00:36:39
It was misinformation and disinformation

00:36:38 --> 00:36:40
that won Donald Trump the election.

00:36:39 --> 00:36:42
We need to pressure the social media companies to start

00:36:41 --> 00:36:45
cracking down on misinformation and disinformation.

00:36:44 --> 00:36:47
And, actually see this in the historical record.

00:36:46 --> 00:36:50
You can see how Jack Dorsey's talk about free speech shifted

00:36:49 --> 00:36:52
from about 2015 to about 2018.

00:36:51 --> 00:36:54
You can see Mark Zuckerberg gave a speech at Georgetown

00:36:53 --> 00:36:57
in 2018; which he talked about free speech in its value.

00:36:56 --> 00:36:58
And by 2019, he was going in front of Congress

00:36:57 --> 00:37:00
talking about how he was responsible for the stuff

00:36:59 --> 00:37:02
that was on Facebook; which is not true.

00:37:01 --> 00:37:04
He's not responsible for the stuff on Facebook.

00:37:03 --> 00:37:05
It's a platform.

00:37:04 --> 00:37:07
Is AT&T responsible for the stuff you say on your phone?

00:37:06 --> 00:37:08
The answer is typically no.

00:37:07 --> 00:37:11
So when that happened, all of these,

00:37:10 --> 00:37:12
because all the eyeballs had now be centralized

00:37:11 --> 00:37:14
in these social media sites,

00:37:13 --> 00:37:15
they were able to suddenly control

00:37:15 --> 00:37:18
what you could see and what you could not see.

00:37:17 --> 00:37:19
And the most obvious example

00:37:18 --> 00:37:22
was obviously leading up to 2020,

00:37:21 --> 00:37:24
the election, the killing of the Hunter Biden story,

00:37:23 --> 00:37:26
is a great example of this.

00:37:25 --> 00:37:28
And so, Elon coming in and taking over one

00:37:27 --> 00:37:29
of the social media services and saying,

00:37:28 --> 00:37:31
"I'm not playing by your rules."

00:37:30 --> 00:37:35
There's not gonna be this sort of group of people

00:37:34 --> 00:37:36
in the halls of power who are gonna decide

00:37:35 --> 00:37:38
what we can see and hear.

00:37:37 --> 00:37:40
Instead, I'm gonna let 1,000 flowers bloom.

00:37:39 --> 00:37:41
There'll be limits,

00:37:40 --> 00:37:43
but it's gonna be on a more case by case basis.

00:37:42 --> 00:37:47
We're going to allow perspectives that are mainstream,

00:37:46 --> 00:37:50
but maybe not mainstream in the halls of academia

00:37:49 --> 00:37:51
or in the halls of media,

00:37:50 --> 00:37:53
let those be said.

00:37:52 --> 00:37:55
I think that's a really good thing.

00:37:54 --> 00:37:57
Now, that comes with, some responsibilities

00:37:56 --> 00:37:59
on Elon's personal part;

00:37:58 --> 00:38:02
which would be, for example,

00:38:01 --> 00:38:04
I think more responsible and dissemination of information

00:38:03 --> 00:38:05
himself sometimes, right.

00:38:04 --> 00:38:07
He got himself in trouble the other day for tweeting out

00:38:06 --> 00:38:09
hat that story about Paul Pelosi,

00:38:08 --> 00:38:12
that that was speculative and and untrue.

00:38:11 --> 00:38:15
And, I don't think what he did is horrific,

00:38:14 --> 00:38:17
he deleted it when he found out that it was false.

00:38:16 --> 00:38:19
And, that's actually a free speech working, right.

00:38:18 --> 00:38:21
He said something wrong, people ripped into him.

00:38:20 --> 00:38:23
He realized he was wrong and he deleted it;

00:38:22 --> 00:38:24
which seems to me a better solution

00:38:23 --> 00:38:26
than preemptively banning content,

00:38:25 --> 00:38:27
which only raises more questions

00:38:26 --> 00:38:29
than than it actually stops.

00:38:28 --> 00:38:33
With that said, as the face of responsible free speech,

00:38:33 --> 00:38:36
and that's sort of what he's pitching at Twitter.

00:38:35 --> 00:38:38
He, I think, should enact that himself

00:38:37 --> 00:38:39
and be a little more careful in the stuff

00:38:38 --> 00:38:40
that he tweets out.

00:38:39 --> 00:38:42
- Well, that's a tricky balance.

00:38:41 --> 00:38:45
The reason a lot of people are freaking out is because one,

00:38:44 --> 00:38:47
he's putting his thumb on the scale

00:38:46 --> 00:38:50
by saying he is more likely to vote Republican.

00:38:49 --> 00:38:53
He's showing himself to be center, right.

00:38:52 --> 00:38:54
And sort of just having a political opinion

00:38:53 --> 00:38:57
versus being this amorphous thing

00:38:56 --> 00:38:59
that doesn't have a political opinion.

00:38:58 --> 00:39:01
I think, if I were to guess,

00:39:00 --> 00:39:02
I haven't talked to him about it,

00:39:01 --> 00:39:04
but if I were to guess, he's sending a kind of signal

00:39:03 --> 00:39:07
that's important for Twitter, the company itself.

00:39:06 --> 00:39:08
Because if we're being honest,

00:39:07 --> 00:39:10
most of the employees are left-leaning.

00:39:09 --> 00:39:12
So you have to kind of send a signal that,

00:39:11 --> 00:39:15
like a resisting mechanism to say,

00:39:15 --> 00:39:18
since most of the employees are left,

00:39:17 --> 00:39:22
it's good for Elon to be more right

00:39:21 --> 00:39:25
o balance out the way the actual engineering is done.

00:39:24 --> 00:39:28
To say, we're not going to do any kind of activism

00:39:27 --> 00:39:29
inside the engineering.

00:39:28 --> 00:39:33
If I were to guess, that's kind of the effective aspect

00:39:32 --> 00:39:35
of that mechanism.

00:39:34 --> 00:39:37
And, the other one, by posting the Pelosi thing

00:39:36 --> 00:39:40
is probably to expand the Overton Window.

00:39:39 --> 00:39:43
Saying we can play,

00:39:42 --> 00:39:44
we could post stuff,

00:39:43 --> 00:39:46
we could post conspiracy theories,

00:39:45 --> 00:39:48
and then through discourse,

00:39:47 --> 00:39:49
figure out what is and isn't true.

00:39:48 --> 00:39:50
- Yeah, again, like I say,

00:39:49 --> 00:39:52
I mean I think that is a better mechanism

00:39:51 --> 00:39:55
in action than what it was before.

00:39:54 --> 00:39:57
I just think it gave people who hate his guts the opening

00:39:56 --> 00:40:00
to kind of slap him for no reason.

00:39:59 --> 00:40:02
But, I can see the strategy of it for sure.

00:40:01 --> 00:40:05
And, I think that the general idea

00:40:04 --> 00:40:07
that he's kind of pushing,

00:40:06 --> 00:40:09
right where the company had pushed left before.

00:40:08 --> 00:40:12
I think that there is actually unilateral polarization

00:40:12 --> 00:40:14
right now in politics,

00:40:13 --> 00:40:15
at least with the regard of social media.

00:40:14 --> 00:40:17
In which one side basically says,

00:40:16 --> 00:40:22
"The solution to disinformation is to shut down free speech

00:40:21 --> 00:40:23
"From the other side."

00:40:22 --> 00:40:25
And the other side is basically like,

00:40:24 --> 00:40:26
people like me are saying,

00:40:25 --> 00:40:29
the solution to disinformation is to let a thousand,

00:40:28 --> 00:40:31
I'd rather have people on the left also being able to

00:40:30 --> 00:40:34
put out stuff that I disagree with than for there to be

00:40:33 --> 00:40:36
anybody who's sort of in charge of these social media

00:40:35 --> 00:40:38
platforms and using them as editorial sites.

00:40:37 --> 00:40:39
There are plenty,

00:40:38 --> 00:40:40
I'm not criticizing MSNBC

00:40:39 --> 00:40:41
for not putting on right wing opinions.

00:40:40 --> 00:40:42
I mean, that's fine.

00:40:41 --> 00:40:44
I run a conservative site.

00:40:43 --> 00:40:47
We're not gonna put up left wing opinions on a wide variety

00:40:46 --> 00:40:48
of issues 'cause we are a conservative site.

00:40:47 --> 00:40:51
But, if you pitch yourself as a platform,

00:40:50 --> 00:40:52
that's a different thing.

00:40:51 --> 00:40:53
If you pitch yourself as the town square,

00:40:52 --> 00:40:55
as Elon likes to call it,

00:40:54 --> 00:40:57
then I think Elon has a better idea of that

00:40:56 --> 00:40:59
han many of the former employees did.

00:40:58 --> 00:41:02
Especially, now that we have that report from The Intercept

00:41:01 --> 00:41:03
suggesting that there are people from Twitter

00:41:02 --> 00:41:05
working with DHS

00:41:04 --> 00:41:07
to monitor disinformation,

00:41:06 --> 00:41:09
and being rather vague about what disinformation meant.

00:41:08 --> 00:41:12
- Yeah, I don't think activism has a place

00:41:11 --> 00:41:15
in what is fundamentally an engineering company

00:41:14 --> 00:41:16
that's building a platform.

00:41:17 --> 00:41:19
The people inside the company

00:41:18 --> 00:41:21
should not be putting a thumb on the scale

00:41:20 --> 00:41:22
of what is and isn't allowed.

00:41:21 --> 00:41:25
You should create a mechanism for the people

00:41:24 --> 00:41:27
to decide what is and isn't allowed.

00:41:26 --> 00:41:32
Do you think Trump should have been removed from Twitter?

00:41:31 --> 00:41:34
Should his account be restored?

00:41:33 --> 00:41:35
- His account should be restored.

00:41:34 --> 00:41:36
And, this is coming from somebody

00:41:35 --> 00:41:38
who really dislikes an enormous number

00:41:37 --> 00:41:40
of Donald Trump's tweets.

00:41:39 --> 00:41:43
Again, he's a very important political personage.

00:41:43 --> 00:41:46
Even if he weren't,

00:41:45 --> 00:41:48
I don't think that he should be banned from Twitter

00:41:47 --> 00:41:50
or Facebook in coordinated fashion.

00:41:49 --> 00:41:52
By the way, I hold that opinion about people

00:41:51 --> 00:41:55
who I think are far worse than Donald Trump.

00:41:55 --> 00:41:58
Everyone knows I'm not an Alex Jones guy.

00:41:57 --> 00:41:59
I don't like Alex Jones.

00:41:58 --> 00:42:00
I think Alex Jones-

00:41:59 --> 00:42:02
You think Alex should be back on Twitter?

00:42:01 --> 00:42:03
- I do actually,

00:42:02 --> 00:42:04
because I think that there are plenty of people

00:42:03 --> 00:42:09
who are willing to say that what he's saying is wrong.

00:42:08 --> 00:42:10
And, I'm not a big fan of this idea

00:42:09 --> 00:42:14
that because people I disagree with,

00:42:13 --> 00:42:15
and people who have personally targeted me.

00:42:14 --> 00:42:16
By the way,

00:42:15 --> 00:42:18
I mean Alex Jones has said some things about me personally

00:42:18 --> 00:42:19
that I'm not real fond of it.

00:42:18 --> 00:42:21
- You guys are not?

00:42:20 --> 00:42:22
- Well, we're not besties.

00:42:21 --> 00:42:24
No, it turns out, yeah.

00:42:23 --> 00:42:25
All I've said is I don't really enjoy his show.

00:42:24 --> 00:42:28
He's said some other stuff about the antichrist and such;

00:42:27 --> 00:42:30
but that's a bit of a different thing I suppose.

00:42:30 --> 00:42:33
Even so... - Yeah.

00:42:32 --> 00:42:35
- I'm just not a big fan of this idea.

00:42:34 --> 00:42:37
I've defended people who have really gone after me

00:42:36 --> 00:42:39
on a personal level, have targeted me.

00:42:40 --> 00:42:44
The town square is online,

00:42:43 --> 00:42:47
banning people from the town square is unpersoning them.

00:42:46 --> 00:42:48
Unless you violated a criminal statute,

00:42:47 --> 00:42:51
you should not be unpersoned in American society,

00:42:51 --> 00:42:53
as a general rule.

00:42:52 --> 00:42:56
That doesn't mean that companies that are not platforms

00:42:55 --> 00:42:58
don't have the ability to respond to you.

00:42:57 --> 00:43:00
I think Adidas is right

00:42:59 --> 00:43:03
o terminate its contract with Kanye, for example,

00:43:02 --> 00:43:04
or with Ye.

00:43:05 --> 00:43:08
But, Twitter ain't Adidas.

00:43:08 --> 00:43:13
- So the way your stance on free speech

00:43:12 --> 00:43:15
to the degree it's possible to achieve on a platform,

00:43:14 --> 00:43:20
like Twitter is you fight bad speech with more speech,

00:43:19 --> 00:43:21
with better speech.

00:43:23 --> 00:43:29
So, if Alex Jones and Trump was allowed back on,

00:43:28 --> 00:43:31
in the coming months and years

00:43:30 --> 00:43:33
leading up to the 2024 election,

00:43:32 --> 00:43:35
you think that's gonna make for a better world

00:43:34 --> 00:43:37
in the long term?

00:43:36 --> 00:43:38
- I think that on the principle

00:43:37 --> 00:43:39
that people should be allowed to do this,

00:43:38 --> 00:43:42
and the alternative being a group of thought bosses

00:43:41 --> 00:43:44
telling us what we can and cannot see, yes.

00:43:43 --> 00:43:46
Do I think in the short term it's gonna mean a lot of things

00:43:45 --> 00:43:48
that I don't like very much?

00:43:47 --> 00:43:51
Sure, them's the class of doing business.

00:43:50 --> 00:43:54
I think that one of the class of freedom

00:43:53 --> 00:43:56
is people doing things that I don't particularly like.

00:43:55 --> 00:43:58
And, I would prefer the freedom

00:43:57 --> 00:44:02
with all the stuff I don't like, than not the freedom.

00:44:01 --> 00:44:05
- Let me linger on the love a little bit.

00:44:04 --> 00:44:08
You and a lot of people are pretty snarky on Twitter;

00:44:07 --> 00:44:11
sometimes to the point of mockery, derision,

00:44:10 --> 00:44:13
even a bit of,

00:44:12 --> 00:44:15
if I were to say bad faith

00:44:14 --> 00:44:16
in the kind of mockery,

00:44:17 --> 00:44:19
and you see it as a war,

00:44:18 --> 00:44:21
like I disagree with both you and Elon on this.

00:44:20 --> 00:44:24
Elon sees Twitter as a war zone,

00:44:23 --> 00:44:27
or at least has saw it that way in the past.

00:44:26 --> 00:44:30
Have you ever considered being nicer on Twitter?

00:44:29 --> 00:44:31
(hearty laughing)

00:44:30 --> 00:44:34
As a voice that a lot of people look up to,

00:44:33 --> 00:44:39
that if Ben Shapiro becomes a little bit more about love

00:44:38 --> 00:44:40
that's gonna inspire a lot of people

00:44:39 --> 00:44:43
or no, is it just too fun for you?

00:44:42 --> 00:44:44
- The answer is yes.

00:44:43 --> 00:44:45
Sure, it's occurred to me.

00:44:44 --> 00:44:46
Let's put it this way,

00:44:45 --> 00:44:47
there are a lot of tweets that actually don't go out

00:44:46 --> 00:44:49
hat I delete.

00:44:48 --> 00:44:50
I'll say that Twitter.

00:44:49 --> 00:44:52
Twitter's new function, that 32nd function

00:44:51 --> 00:44:53
is a friend of mine.

00:44:52 --> 00:44:54
Every so often,

00:44:53 --> 00:44:56
I'll tweet something and I'll think about it a second.

00:44:55 --> 00:44:57
I'll be like,

00:44:56 --> 00:44:58
"Do I need to say this?"

00:44:57 --> 00:44:58
Probably not.

00:44:57 --> 00:45:02
- Can you make a book published

00:45:01 --> 00:45:07
after you pass away of all the tweets that you didn't send?

00:45:06 --> 00:45:09
- Oh no, my kids are still gonna be around it.

00:45:08 --> 00:45:11
I hope, so that's the legacy.

00:45:10 --> 00:45:14
But yeah, I mean, sure, the answer is yes.

00:45:13 --> 00:45:15
And there's a good piece of what we would call

00:45:14 --> 00:45:17
in Orthodox Judaism, Mussar.

00:45:16 --> 00:45:18
This is like,

00:45:17 --> 00:45:19
he is giving a Mussar schmooze, right now.

00:45:18 --> 00:45:22
This is like the kind of, be a better person stuff.

00:45:21 --> 00:45:23
I agree with you.

00:45:22 --> 00:45:25
I agree with you, and yeah.

00:45:24 --> 00:45:27
I will say that Twitter is sometimes too much fun.

00:45:26 --> 00:45:31
I try to be at least,

00:45:30 --> 00:45:32
if not even-handed,

00:45:31 --> 00:45:36
then equal opportunity in my derision.

00:45:35 --> 00:45:38
I remember that during the 2016 primaries,

00:45:37 --> 00:45:41
I used to post rather snarky tweets

00:45:40 --> 00:45:42
about virtually all of the candidates,

00:45:41 --> 00:45:45
Republican and Democrat.

00:45:44 --> 00:45:48
And, every so often I'll still do some of that.

00:45:47 --> 00:45:50
I do think actually the amount of snark on my Twitter feed

00:45:49 --> 00:45:51
has gone down fairly significantly.

00:45:50 --> 00:45:52
I think if you go back a couple of years,

00:45:51 --> 00:45:54
it was probably a little more snarky.

00:45:53 --> 00:45:56
Today, I'm trying to use it a little bit more

00:45:55 --> 00:45:58
in terms of strategy to get out information.

00:45:57 --> 00:46:01
Now, that doesn't mean I'm not gonna make jokes about,

00:46:00 --> 00:46:05
for example, Joe Biden.

00:46:04 --> 00:46:06
I will make jokes about Joe Biden.

00:46:05 --> 00:46:08
He's the president of the United States.

00:46:07 --> 00:46:09
Nobody else will mock him.

00:46:08 --> 00:46:11
So, the entire comedic establishment has decided

00:46:10 --> 00:46:13
they actually work for him.

00:46:12 --> 00:46:14
- So the president of the United States,

00:46:13 --> 00:46:17
no matter who they are, get the snark.

00:46:16 --> 00:46:18
- Yes, yes.

00:46:17 --> 00:46:20
And, President Trump, I think is fairly aware

00:46:19 --> 00:46:22
that he got the snark from me as well.

00:46:21 --> 00:46:23
